Stability of Axion-Saxion wormholes
T. Hertog, S. Maenaut, B. Missoni, R. Tielemans, T. Van Riet

Abstract
We reconsider the perturbative stability of Euclidean axion wormholes. The quadratic action that governs linear perturbations is derived directly in Euclidean gravity. We demonstrate explicitly that a stability analysis in which one treats the axion as a normal two-form gauge field is equivalent to one performed in the Hodge-dual formulation, where one considers the axion as a scalar with a wrong-sign kinetic term. Both analyses indicate that axion wormholes are perturbatively stable, even in the presence of a massless dilaton, or saxion, field that couples to the axion.



Institute for Theoretical Physics, KU Leuven,

Celestijnenlaan 200D, 3001 Leuven, Belgium

Contents

1
Introduction

For many years, the study of Euclidean wormhole solutions in semi-classical gravity theories has proven useful to gain some understanding of certain non-perturbative properties of quantum gravity.

The so-called Giddings-Strominger wormholes
[
2
]
, where the wormhole throat is supported by axion-flux, are perhaps the prime examples of Euclidean wormhole solutions
[
3
,
4
,
5
,
6
]
1
1

1

For a review of the vast literature on this subject see
[
7
]
, and
[
8
]
for very recent applications.
. In recent years, more general solutions were found in bottom-up models, including wormholes sourced by multiple fields such as massive dilatons
[
9
,
10
,
11
]
, and higher-derivative corrections
[
12
,
13
]
. In parallel, axion wormholes and variations thereof were embedded in string theory and in the AdS/CFT setting
[
14
,
15
,
16
,
17
,
18
,
19
]
2
2

2

See
[
20
]
for a compendium on holographic backgrounds sourced by axion densities.
. These embeddings sharpened the paradoxes that such wormhole-saddles of a gravitational path integral appear to induce. These paradoxes range from the factorisation problem
[
21
]
and the absence of Coleman’s
\alpha
-parameters in AdS/CFT duals
[
22
]
, to certain Swampland principles
[
23
]
and a violation of operator positivity in dual CFTs
[
24
,
14
]
. Quite clearly, a resolution of these paradoxes will require a better understanding of the wormhole solutions, in particular under what conditions they are stable.

Euclidean axion wormholes either connect two different universes, or different regions within the same universe. Here we concentrate on rotationally-invariant solutions in flat space, which is, just like anti-de Sitter (AdS) space, a setting where wormholes connect different universes. This is in contrast with Euclidean de Sitter (dS) space, where wormholes connect opposite sides of the same Euclidean sphere
[
25
,
26
]
, yielding a kettle-bell-like geometry. In this case, wormhole configurations can contribute to the Hartle-Hawking quantum state
[
26
]
.

Building on recent work
[
1
,
27
]
, we study the perturbative stability of axion wormholes in the presence of a dilaton field, or saxion. As argued long ago by Coleman
[
28
]
, the existence (and number) of negative modes around saddle points in Euclidean quantum gravity determines their nature and interpretation. If there are no negative modes, one expects the saddle point to lift a vacuum degeneracy or contribute to the wave function of the universe. Saddles and boundary conditions for which there is a single negative mode describe tunnelling transitions. Finally, the presence of a multitude of negative modes indicates that the saddle point in question may not provide a physically meaningful or relevant contribution to the gravitational path integral.

Originally Coleman
[
5
]
suggested that axion wormholes describe tunnelling events in which baby universes pinch of from or are absorbed by the mother universe. This would lead to a violation of axion charge conservation from the viewpoint of the mother universe, consistent with the expectation that gravity eliminates global symmetries through wormholes
[
29
]
. From this perspective wormholes contribute to matrix elements described by path integrals with boundary conditions that keep the axion charge fixed.
In this context,
[
1
,
30
]
argued that axion wormholes have multiple negative modes, and this was interpreted as evidence that e.g. the factorisation problem may not be a problem after all. However, subsequently
[
27
]
studied the stability of these wormholes based on a different treatment of the axion perturbations, and found no negative modes. Whereas
[
1
]
worked with the scalar field formulation of the axion, the analysis of
[
27
]
made use of the Hodge-dual formulation, where the axion is a two-form field. Since Hodge duality frames cannot change physical results, here we reconsider the analysis of
[
1
]
. We identify a problem with the boundary condition used in
[
1
]
and show in detail how the boundary condition that the axion charge remains fixed, when implemented correctly, resolves the discrepancy between the two Hodge dual formulations in
[
1
]
and
[
27
]
. Along the way we generalise the stability analysis to axion wormhole saddles that include a massless dilaton field, since this is expected on general grounds in string theory embeddings of these solutions
[
15
,
16
,
14
]
.

Note added:
While this paper was in the final stages of completion,
[
11
]
appeared which verified the stability of the homogeneous sector. Here we consider all modes.

2
Axion-Saxion wormholes

We consider wormhole solutions of Euclidean gravity coupled to an axion field. An axion
\chi
is a scalar which enjoys a classical shift symmetry
\chi\rightarrow\chi+c
. It can be dualised to a two-form field
B_{\mu\nu}
in four dimensions with field strength
F=dB
related to
\chi
via
\star_{4}F_{3}=\pm d\chi
. In Euclidean signature Hodge duality can be subtle, however, since axions can come with a ‘wrong-sign’ kinetic term, depending on what boundary conditions one considers. In what follows we are interested in boundary conditions that keep the axion charge
Q
or better, the axion flux, fixed:

Q=\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{\Sigma}F.

(2.1)

where
\Sigma
is a hypersurface at infinity.

The metric of wormhole solutions that preserve rotational symmetry can be written in the form

\displaystyle\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s^{2}=N^{2}(r)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}r^{2}+a^{2}(r)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\Omega_{3}^{2},

(2.2)

where
\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\Omega_{3}^{2}
is the metric on
S^{3}
. The field strength is then of the form

F=Q\mathcal{E},

(2.3)

where
\mathcal{E}
is the volume form on
S^{3}
. The equations of motion can be deduced from the following action:

S[B,g]=\int\left[-\frac{1}{2\kappa_{4}^{2}}\star\mathcal{R}+\frac{1}{2}\star dB\wedge dB\right]

(2.4)

The
B
-field equation is solved by our Ansatz above and the Einstein equation reduces to

\left(\frac{\dot{a}}{N}\right)^{2}=1-\frac{\kappa_{4}^{2}Q^{2}}{6}a^{-4},

(2.5)

where
\dot{a}=\partial_{r}a
. In conformal gauge
(N=a)
the solution reads
a^{2}=\frac{\kappa_{4}|Q|}{\sqrt{6}}\cosh(2r)
. The inclusion of a cosmological constant in (
2.4
) is straightforward, see e.g.
[
25
]
.

To exhibit the choice of boundary conditions clearly at the level of the action, it is useful to write the action as a function of
F
,

S[F,\chi,g]=\int\left[-\frac{1}{2\kappa_{4}^{2}}\star\mathcal{R}+\frac{1}{2}\star F\wedge F+\chi\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}F\right].

(2.6)

Here
\chi
is a Lagrange multiplier that enforces
dF=0
such that, locally,
F=dB
. This action clearly reproduces the same equations of motion, but has the benefit that it allows a clean implementation of the above boundary condition as a Dirichlet condition on
F
. Alternatively, one can integrate out
F
and leave it as a function of
\chi
, which is the proper way to carry out Hodge dualisation at the level of the action. This yields

F=-\star\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\chi,

(2.7)

which, when inserted in the action gives

S[\chi,g]=\int_{\mathcal{M}}\left[-\frac{1}{2\kappa_{4}^{2}}\mathcal{R}-\frac{1}{2}\star\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\chi\wedge\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\chi\right]-\int_{\partial\mathcal{M}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\left(\chi\star\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\chi\right).

(2.8)

The
\chi
-field has the wrong-sign kinetic term. Yet, the action is well-defined because of the boundary term, which renders the non-gravitational part bounded from below.
In this Hodge-dual form, the Noether charge of the shift symmetry gives the axion flux

Q=(2\pi^{2})^{-1}\int_{\Sigma}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x\sqrt{\gamma}\>\pi
, where
\pi=\partial^{0}\chi
is the Euclidean momentum of
\chi
.

In the context of unified theories such as string theory axions tend to be part of a complex scalar. The partner field
\varphi
, the dilaton or saxion, does not enjoy a shift symmetry, but typically has a Lagrangian of the form

\mathcal{L}=-\frac{1}{2}\star d\varphi\wedge d\varphi+\frac{1}{2}e^{b\varphi}\star d\chi\wedge d\chi+d(e^{b\varphi}\chi\star d\chi).

(2.9)

If the dilaton-coupling
b
is sufficiently small,

b^{2}<\frac{8}{3}\kappa_{4}^{2}\ ,

(2.10)

then this axion-dilaton theory has regular wormhole solutions
[
22
]
. An example is type II string theory on a six-torus, which reduces to maximal supergravity in 4 dimensions. This admits a consistent truncation of the moduli space such that one obtains the above axion-dilaton model with a coupling
b
in the range (
2.10
)
[
31
,
14
]
. This shows that regular axion-saxion wormhole solutions can be embedded in string theory.

In general, supersymmetric compactifications of string theory lead to general sigma models coupled to gravity with Lagrangian

\mathcal{L}=-\frac{1}{2}G_{IJ}(\Phi)\star d\Phi^{I}\wedge d\Phi^{J}+\ldots

(2.11)

where
G_{IJ}
is the moduli metric and the dots represent total derivatives. Once again there are symmetric instanton solutions of the form (
2.2
). In these, the scalars trace out geodesics in the target space with metric
G_{IJ}
, parametrized by an affine coordinate
h(r)

[
32
]
. The latter is nothing but the solution for the radial harmonic on (
2.2
):

\partial_{r}(a^{3}N^{-1}\partial_{r}h(r))=0\quad\rightarrow\quad\frac{dh}{dr}=Na^{-3}\,.

(2.12)

The total geodesic velocity is a constant

G_{IJ}(\Phi)\frac{d}{dh}\Phi^{I}\frac{d}{dh}\Phi^{J}=c\,,

(2.13)

where
c
must be negative in order to have a regular wormhole. In effect, using this geodesic behaviour one recovers the single-axion equation (
2.5
) for the scale factor with
Q^{2}=-c
.
For the two-field model
2.9
, the explicit solutions for the geodesics are:

e^{b\varphi/2}=\frac{|Q|}{\sqrt{-c}}\cos\left(\frac{b\sqrt{-c}}{2}\,h(r)\right)\,,\qquad\chi=\frac{2\sqrt{-c}}{bQ}\tan\left(\frac{b\sqrt{-c}}{2}\,h(r)\right)\,.

(2.14)

The regularity condition (
2.10
) follows from the requirement that
e^{b\varphi/2}
should not change sign as
h(r)
varies over the entire wormhole background
3
3

3

This requirement might seem rather ad hoc. Note, however, that on general grounds one interprets the dilaton coupling to
(\partial\chi)^{2}
as the squared coupling constant of the axion two-form
B
. This is confirmed explicitly in the top-down constructions of axion wormholes in flat space
[
14
]
where
e^{b\varphi/2}
represents the physical size of certain cycles inside the compactification space and hence are required to remain positive.
. In conformal gauge the harmonic function reads (up to an additive constant),

h(r)=\frac{\sqrt{6}}{2\kappa_{4}\sqrt{-c}}\arctan\left(\sinh(2r)\right)\ ,

(2.15)

which, when substituted in (
2.14
), yields the regularity condition (
2.10
).

3
Perturbations

3.1
Perturbed action

To study whether axionic wormholes can be relevant saddle point contributions to the gravitational path integral, we now analyse the stability of the solutions in the two-field model with respect to small fluctuations. To this end we expand the action up to quadratic order in perturbations in both the metric and the (s)axion fields.
We set
\kappa_{4}=1
from now on and consider first the action in terms of the three-form field strength, viz.

\displaystyle S_{E}

\displaystyle=\int\differential[4]{x}\sqrt{g}\left(-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{R}+\frac{1}{12}e^{-b\varphi}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.17293pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83778pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.17293pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83778pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.17293pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83778pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.17293pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83778pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu\sigma}}}}\mathchoice{F^{{{\mu\nu\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.17293pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83778pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{{\mu\nu\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.17293pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83778pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{{\mu\nu\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.17293pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83778pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{{\mu\nu\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.50554pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[8.17293pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.83778pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}+\frac{1}{2}\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\varphi\mathchoice{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\varphi+\frac{1}{6}\chi\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.7063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.64732pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.7063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.64732pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.7063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.64732pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[17.65453pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[10.7063pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.64732pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu\rho\sigma}}}}\right).

(3.1)

As discussed above, the Lagrange multiplier in (
3.1
) ensures that this is equivalent to the action in the scalar field formulation with the appropriate total derivative added,

\displaystyle S_{E}

\displaystyle=\int\differential[4]{x}\sqrt{g}\left(-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{R}-\frac{1}{2}e^{b\varphi}\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\chi\mathchoice{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi+\frac{1}{2}\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\varphi\mathchoice{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\varphi+\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\left(e^{b\varphi}\chi\mathchoice{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\nabla^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\chi\right)\right).

(3.2)

In this section we perform a stability analysis of the wormholes in both the three-form and scalar-field formulations of the theory. We demonstrate explicitly that the results of both calculations agree, provided boundary conditions are treated carefully.

The background equations of motion are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{2}

\displaystyle=k+\frac{1}{6}\left(\dot{\varphi}^{2}-e^{b\varphi}\dot{\chi}^{2}\right)=k+\frac{1}{6}\left(\dot{\varphi}^{2}-e^{-b\varphi}Q^{2}/a^{4}\right),

\displaystyle\dot{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=-2\mathcal{H}^{2}+2k,

(3.3)

\displaystyle\ddot{\varphi}

\displaystyle=-2\,\mathcal{H}\,\dot{\varphi}-\frac{1}{2}\,b\,e^{b\varphi}\,\dot{\chi}^{2}=-2\,\mathcal{H}\,\dot{\varphi}-\frac{1}{2}\,b\,e^{-b\varphi}\,Q^{2}/a^{4},

\displaystyle\ddot{\chi}

\displaystyle=-2\mathcal{H}\dot{\chi}-b\dot{\varphi}\dot{\chi}.

(3.4)

where
\mathcal{H}
is the conformal Hubble rate
\dot{a}/a
and we have kept for now the three-space curvature
k
general.

The theory of perturbations around a wormhole background of the form (
2.2
) is analogous to cosmological perturbation theory in Euclidean signature (see e.g.
[
33
,
34
]
). There and here, it is useful to decompose a general metric perturbation in so-called scalar, vector and tensor components, which to linear order evolve independently. Since the vector and tensor components aren’t sourced by scalar fields, they aren’t potential sources of instability. Thus we concentrate here on scalar metric perturbations. In conformal gauge, the wormhole metric perturbed by a general scalar perturbation reads

\displaystyle\differential{s}^{2}

\mathchoice{\xi^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\xi^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\xi^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\xi^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}=\left(\mathchoice{\xi^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\xi^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\xi^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\xi^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}},\mathchoice{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\xi\right)
, these fluctuations transform as

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\to f+Q\triangle{\xi},

\displaystyle w

\displaystyle\to w+Q\mathchoice{\dot{\xi}}{\dot{\xi}}{\dot{\xi}}{\dot{\xi}}.

(3.8)

The perturbative stability of wormholes depends on the quadratic action operator and on the Dirichlet boundary conditions on the axion charge and dilaton fluctuation. Since all physically meaningful statements are gauge-invariant, we demand the same from our boundary conditions. Perturbations of the axion charge and of the dilaton field transform under gauge transformations as

\displaystyle\delta Q_{\text{axion}}

\displaystyle\to\delta Q_{\text{axion}}+\int_{S^{3}}\differential[3]{x}\sqrt{\mathchoice{\gamma}{\gamma}{\gamma}{\gamma}}Q\triangle{\xi}

\displaystyle\delta\mathchoice{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}

\displaystyle\to\delta\mathchoice{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}+\mathchoice{\xi^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\xi^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\xi^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\xi^{{{0}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\dot{\varphi}.

(3.9)

The integral over the three-sphere vanishes since its integrand is a total derivative and the three-sphere is a compact manifold without a boundary. The term with
\dot{\varphi}
also vanishes since
\varphi
asymptotically approaches a constant value. Thus, our boundary conditions are gauge-invariant and we can safely proceed with our analysis.

(3.11)

For the axion field, it is useful to demonstrate explicitly that the perturbed action derived from (
3.1
) is equivalent to the one derived from (
3.2
). Starting with the former, the perturbed action up to quadratic order in the perturbations is given by

\displaystyle\delta^{2}S_{E}^{\text{axion,F}}

(3.12)

(3.13)

Starting with the scalar-field formulation of the axion action (
3.2
), the second-order perturbed action reads

\displaystyle\delta^{2}S_{E}^{\text{axion,$\chi$}}=\int\differential[4]{x}\sqrt{\mathchoice{g}{g}{g}{g}}\bigg[

Note that this requires keeping all contributions of the second-order perturbations of
\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu\rho\sigma}}}}
that contain products of
\delta\mathchoice{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.8716pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.373pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.83784pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu\nu}}}}
and the scalars
\delta\mathchoice{\chi}{\chi}{\chi}{\chi},\delta\mathchoice{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}
, which form a total derivative. Also, although contributions of
\delta^{2}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu\rho\sigma}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.7922pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.75381pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.5384pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu\rho\sigma}}}}
are multiplied by background expressions that are zero, these must nevertheless be taken in account to show the equivalence of the perturbed action under the Hodge duality, since they lead to terms that include the product of two first-order perturbations.

Expressing the three-form fluctuations as in (
3.6
) and restricting to the scalar metric perturbations of (
3.5
), the quadratic action (
3.12
), up to a total derivative on the induced metric, becomes

\displaystyle\delta^{2}S_{E}^{axion,F}=\int\differential{r}\differential[3]{x}\,\frac{\sqrt{\gamma}}{a^{2}}\bigg[

\displaystyle e^{-b\varphi}\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\left(f^{2}+\mathchoice{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}w\mathchoice{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}w\right)+Q\left(f\left(\mathchoice{A}{A}{A}{A}+3\mathchoice{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}-\triangle\mathchoice{E}{E}{E}{E}-b\delta\mathchoice{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}\right)+w\triangle\mathchoice{B}{B}{B}{B}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}Q^{2}\Big(2b\delta\mathchoice{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}\left(\mathchoice{A}{A}{A}{A}+3\mathchoice{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}-\triangle\mathchoice{E}{E}{E}{E}\right)-b^{2}\delta\mathchoice{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}^{2}+\mathchoice{A}{A}{A}{A}^{2}-15\mathchoice{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}^{2}-6\mathchoice{A}{A}{A}{A}\mathchoice{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}+2\mathchoice{B}{B}{B}{B}\triangle\mathchoice{B}{B}{B}{B}+2\mathchoice{A}{A}{A}{A}\triangle\mathchoice{E}{E}{E}{E}

w\right)\bigg].

(3.15)

), excluding the final boundary term, equals

\displaystyle\delta^{2}S_{E}^{axion,\chi}=\int\differential{r}\differential[3]{x}\,a^{2}\sqrt{\gamma}e^{b\varphi}\Big(

\displaystyle\dot{\chi}\left(\delta\mathchoice{\dot{\chi}}{\dot{\chi}}{\dot{\chi}}{\dot{\chi}}\left(\mathchoice{A}{A}{A}{A}+3\mathchoice{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}-\triangle\mathchoice{E}{E}{E}{E}-b\delta\mathchoice{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}\right)+\mathchoice{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}\delta\mathchoice{\chi}{\chi}{\chi}{\chi}\mathchoice{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{B}{B}{B}{B}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left((\dot{\chi})^{2}+\mathchoice{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt] \displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}(\dot{\chi})^{2}\big(2b\delta\mathchoice{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}\left(\mathchoice{A}{A}{A}{A}+3\mathchoice{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}-\triangle\mathchoice{E}{E}{E}{E}\right)+b^{2}\delta\mathchoice{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}{\varphi}^{2}+3\mathchoice{A}{A}{A}{A}^{2}+3\mathchoice{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}^{2}+6\mathchoice{A}{A}{A}{A}\mathchoice{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}

\displaystyle-2\mathchoice{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}{\psi}\triangle\mathchoice{E}{E}{E}{E}-2\mathchoice{A}{A}{A}{A}\triangle\mathchoice{E}{E}{E}{E}+\mathchoice{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}}}}\mathchoice{B}{B}{B}{B}\mathchoice{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{D^{{{i}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{B}{B}{B}{B}+\triangle\mathchoice{E}{E}{E}{E}\triangle\mathchoice{E}{E}{E}{E}-2\mathchoice{D^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{
